# Research audit status

The existing figures and analytical conclusions are **legacy, not revalidated findings**.
The current changes repair infrastructure and preserve evidence; they do not certify a
replacement classifier or establish complete contemporary coverage.

**October 20, 2026 continuation:** latest completed audit **v54** binds **126 research
and accounting packets**. The canonical audit has **308/342 passing checks**
and **34 missing-artifact failures**. Known source, collection and scientific failures
remain explicit; this is not a completed project or a certified classifier.

Virginia's bounded initial source packet now passes independent original-verifier
reconstruction: **6,458 distinct introduced identities**, 6,698 raw rows including
240 duplicates, twelve original HTML contexts and two PDFs / four pages per reader.
All 125 physical exchanges and all 697 packet file/directory metadata records
reconcile unchanged. **166 canonical-only 2023 identities remain unresolved**;
the introduced observations are not certification of the complete carryover/pending
business universe, and 6,446 native originals remain unrecovered. Georgia stopped
after four exchanges when its official sessions endpoint returned HTTP 401.
Independent replay accepts its blocked-evidence archive only, with all 64 packet
file/directory records unchanged: no native census or original was recovered.
Its late original-metadata capture and construction-phase byte-restoration incidents
remain disclosed; current equality does not establish acquisition-time immutability.

Two fresh **GPT-6.1 Sol** agents now own bounded Minnesota and New Jersey recovery,
each limited to 128 aggregate physical official exchanges including all discovered
history, retries, redirects and failures. New Jersey must preserve the publisher's
actual biennial boundaries rather than invent a 2023-2024 native session. Neither
packet has returned or been accepted. Pennsylvania's narrower offline-verification
worker remains responsible for its distinct immutable successor. Its exact v6 parser
independently rejects the three numeric-whitespace errors, but nine required-denial
omissions still block readiness acceptance. All 32 payloads and 36 original
file/directory metadata records remain unchanged. No actual campaign resumed.

North Carolina's first independent initialization reconciliation failed on the
parent's proof-copy role ordering. Corrected v2 passed those copy checks but failed
on another parent assumption: initialization legitimately inherits sixteen transport
artifacts from two reused listing-page exchanges, so zero new HTTP does not imply
an empty transport directory. Both failed sources and logs are preserved. Distinct
v3 now passes independent reconciliation: all 44,342 initialized files match,
all 56,761 original file/directory records remain unchanged, and the sixteen
inherited transport files match their frozen paths and hashes with no new HTTP.
Raw proof, generated source, receipt and complete original map bytes also reconcile.
Native-version collection remains unauthorized; the unlaunched finite wrapper still
references the superseded v1 receipt rather than this accepted successor.

Local timeout fixtures independently reproduce three problems in that old finite
wrapper: an absent-group signal race raises, a TERM-ignoring descendant survives,
and a timed-out child can exit zero and satisfy the enclosing success condition.
A candidate cleanup using verified, explicitly owned positive PIDs now passes
the bounded local fixtures, including descendant termination and graceful exit.
Two earlier process-group-signal permission failures remain preserved. This is a
local cleanup proof, not an accepted replacement launcher: receipt rebinding,
timeout-failure status and source-dependent launch controls still need integration.
No collector or HTTP ran during these checks.

## Organization profiles: missing evidence and undefined outlier comparisons

Actual pre-fix execution records **16 failing regressions**, including real CLI
exports that cannot be read back because they contain no headers. The expanded
20-case suite also fails against the actual SHA-pinned saved baseline
`a09d63b`, not a reimplementation of it. Logs:
`profile_missingness_before_v1.log` and
`profile_missingness_saved_baseline_before_v2.log`.

All three profile/outlier CSVs now retain their schemas, including empty,
single-state, below-floor and entirely null-filtered populations; new runs
replace stale outputs. Gap-only organizations are included rather than silently
dropped. Missing gap metadata and unknown document counts remain nullable,
distinct from an observed zero. The CLI identifies each D/R comparison with
fewer than two eligible states as **unavailable**, not evidence of conformity.

Unknown, nonfinite or negative topic counts raise explicitly instead of being
silently summed into a profile. A zero-total group is not a topic-share vector,
even with a zero sample floor. Outlier inputs must be finite, nonnegative
topic-share distributions summing to one; undefined inputs no longer manufacture
distance 1 or NaN results. Nonpositive null-draw configuration also raises.
The existing leave-one-out equal-state peer mean, sample floors, multinomial
null calculation, random seeds, tie handling and rounding are unchanged.

All **92 targeted emphasis/intra-party/state-focus tests**, Ruff and diff
checks pass in `profile_missingness_complete_v2.log`. An actual old/new
differential executes 36 synthetic platform rows, 18 bill rows and six gap rows.
Current-era and pooled-era profiles, three topic-vector populations, and six
raw/null-filtered outlier comparisons agree exactly, including dtypes, column
order and CSV bytes. Both real CLIs produce identical supported stdout after
normalizing only their distinct output paths, and all three CSV exports match
byte-for-byte. Receipt `profile_missingness_supported_differential_v1.json`,
SHA `ab970efb06c3c94ad8a9c3272f19484fdff64ebdb6f699f81a812f55143b64b4`.
Temporary synthetic inputs/outputs are removed. No actual research outputs,
source evidence, labels, model weights, scientific admission or publication
files change.

## Outcome inference availability and nullable eligibility

Actual pre-fix execution records **eleven failures and four passing controls** in
`outcome_inference_missingness_before_v1.log`. Zero or one paired state returns
1.0 as though inference were available; zero permutations also produces 1.0.
Nullable classifications raise an ambiguous-truth-value error, a fresh CLI
output directory is not created, and the artifact auditor crashes on a null
probability. Existing report/chart formatting cannot represent unavailable
inference consistently.

Sign-flip inference now returns JSON **null** for fewer than two paired states,
and nonpositive permutation counts raise an explicit configuration error.
Observed one-state differences, including zero, remain descriptive values;
their unavailable inference is not replaced with a chance result. A shared
formatter serves the chart and figure report and suppresses legacy one-state
1.0 values even when reading an older comparison file. The auditor records a
failed probability check for null instead of raising a TypeError.

Missing classifications do not become ordinary bills. An explicit boolean mask
preserves columns for empty eligible populations, the CLI creates its output
directory, and all four CSVs remain header-readable. Comparison JSON uses strict
serialization. The recorded-stage estimand, all-eligible filing denominators,
numerators, coverage floors, seed and supported permutation calculation are
unchanged.

All **85 targeted outcome/plotting/report/null-auditor tests**, Ruff and diff
checks pass in `outcome_inference_missingness_complete_v1.log`. An actual
old/new differential against saved revision
`d0fb7388c5d038323696268e6a82098e7b4f0e4b` executes a 6,005-row synthetic
bill fixture, 24 vote events, 96 party-count rows and ten supported sign-flip
arrays. All four frames, dtypes, column order and CSV bytes match exactly.
Both real CLI executions also produce identical stdout, all four CSVs and the
comparison JSON. Receipt: `outcome_inference_supported_differential_v1.json`,
SHA `d25dcefad38183786ac4b30d0a378c5f02762b9cfa89f33c166d92bb4612bd1e`.
The temporary fixture is removed. No actual research outputs, source evidence,
labels, model weights, scientific admission or publication files change.

## Bill-tag diagnostics: prediction-only classes and unavailable rates

Actual pre-fix synthetic execution records **six failures and two passing
controls** in `tag_agreement_zero_support_before_v1.log`. The report previously
iterated only over tag-reference classes: predictions assigned exclusively to
another class disappeared from its per-topic false-positive/precision accounting.
The figure report also printed `nan%` for classes with no predictions.

The diagnostic now retains the union of reference and predicted classes within
the same eligible mapped-tag population. Prediction-only classes retain their
predicted counts, **zero measured tag precision** and contaminants; their absent
reference denominators yield unavailable recall/coverage, not invented zeros.
Reference-only classes retain their measured recall and unavailable precision.
Abstentions, Montana source-draft exclusions and unmapped-tag exclusions remain
unchanged. Overall eligible counts and agreement numerators do not increase.
The CLI renders unavailable rates without `nan%`; the figure report ranks only

## Intra-party missingness, undefined ratios and empty exports

Direct execution exposes **eleven initial regression failures**, with one passing
numeric control, in `intraparty_missingness_before_v1.log`. Empty vectors lack
named result schemas; no shared states or only one shared state cause sorting
failures before exports finish. Empty runs skip writing outputs, allowing old
results to survive a new unsupported population. When between-party distance is
exactly zero, the ratio is undefined and CLI formatting raises rather than
preserving the observed zero distances.

A separate failure is substantive: undefined cosine distances produce an
undefined observed dispersion gap, but every NaN comparison is false and the
permutation formula still returns a small numeric p-value. The repaired test
requires finite, nonzero organization vectors before reporting a permutation
result and rejects zero permutations as invalid configuration. This does not
change the permutation design or its computation on supported vectors.

All four table-producing helpers now retain their existing column schemas for
empty results. The CLI always writes all eight CSV artifacts, including readable
empty replacements for stale files, and explicitly reports insufficient shared
samples. A shared formatter preserves observed zero distances and displays
undefined ratios as **unavailable** in both the CLI and figure report. The
report also treats a legacy null gap/p-value as unavailable, not a finding
indistinguishable from chance; that additional pre-fix failure is preserved in
`intraparty_missingness_report_gap_before_v1.log`.

The final **68 targeted intra-party/plotting/report tests**, Ruff and diff checks
pass: `intraparty_missingness_complete_v3.log`. Earlier successful v1/v2 logs
remain retained. Regression cases include empty, one-party, one-shared-state,
zero-between-distance and wholly/partially undefined vector populations, plus
replacement of stale output files.

## Classifier numerical decision safeguards

Five executed regressions expose a shared decision-path failure: individually
finite logits and an allowed positive finite temperature can overflow during
scaling, yielding NaN scores. Comparisons against confidence and margin gates
then fail to reject them; a mixed or supervised-rejection input can incorrectly
receive a policy topic. These are synthetic numerical counterexamples, not
evidence that any saved pilot prediction encountered overflow.

The shared decision path now centers the original logits before scaling **only
when the normal scaled values are non-finite**. This produces finite normalized
scores and preserves the mixed-chunk and learned-rejection decisions. The
ordinary finite-scaling calculation is unchanged, as are source inputs,
weights, labels, thresholds, calibration files and scientific status.
All **87 ModernBERT safeguard tests** and targeted Ruff checks pass. The actual
five-case pre-fix failures and completed run remain in
`modernbert_finite_scores_before_v1.log` and
`modernbert_finite_scores_complete_v1.log`. This repairs invalid decisions; it
does not improve measured pilot accuracy or certify the classifier.

## Comparison missingness and matched-state accounting

Direct production inspection and five executed pre-fix regressions reproduced
missing-party bill shares becoming zero, explicit null shares becoming zero,
missing-topic counts remaining null despite a known classified sample, pooled
platform sensitivity inventing a missing party's share, and a global union
state count being reported for each party. The failed run is retained as
`comparison_missingness_before_v1.log`; the original production files and
regression inputs are bound by `comparison_missingness_before_v1.sha256`.

The comparison now distinguishes an absent topic within an observed classified
party sample from an unavailable sample or an explicitly unknown observation.
Only the former receives a zero share and, for bills, a zero topic count.
The output retains each observed topic for both major parties, including
bill-only topics and their source-provided names; conflicting names for the
same topic code fail explicitly. An unavailable side leaves the numerical gap
unavailable. The upstream pooled platform summary and comparison CLI no longer
reintroduce missing-party zeros. Equal-state and pooled bill summaries report
the number of observed states for each party, not the union across parties.

The subject-tag replication had a separate unconditional zero-fill and omitted
model-state availability from its matched-state intersection. It now reuses
the comparison rules across the union of all three streams' topics and matches
states with both D/R observations in platforms, model-labelled bills and
tag-labelled bills. Its `holds` verdict is nullable: an unavailable source
share produces an unavailable verdict, not a failed replication. CSV exports
retain the missing value; the reporting script separately counts unavailable
rows and uses only available verdicts in its replication denominator. Plotting
preserves missing markers and does not flag unavailable verdicts as
contradictions. Known ties still do not support a nonzero directional claim.

All **82** tests in the three affected emphasis, bill-validation and plotting
suites pass, including actual temporary-file CLI/export checks with classifier
test doubles, an empty matched-state case, nullable report denominators and
masked plotted observations. Targeted Ruff checks also pass. The actual final
run is retained as `comparison_missingness_complete_v1.log`. No production
inference, training, label edits or analytical regeneration was performed.
These are accounting repairs, not improved accuracy measurements or new
substantive findings; the v53 audit and frozen legacy outputs remain unchanged.

## Shared retrieval response lifecycle and evidence limits

After the v43 checkpoint, the shared `fetch` path was repaired to close successful,
HTTP-error, retryable and oversized responses after reading or rejecting them,
before another attempt or return. A redirect without a `Location` is no longer
returned already closed by the default transport; its caller owns final cleanup.
This fixes a resource-lifecycle gap without treating an error response as data or
changing retry decisions and source-admission rules.

The helper's receipts summarize logical retrievals, **not every physical request**.
It still does not retain intermediate redirect/retry bodies or HTTP-error bodies,
and its attempt count is not a cross-attempt HTTP request budget. The code guide
and module documentation now state these limits explicitly. Separate bounded
research collectors must continue retaining per-exchange headers/bodies and
enforcing their aggregate allowances; this repair cannot recover previously lost
response bytes or retroactively make earlier collection compliant.

## California complete native archive and introduced-source candidates

The bounded recovery below has been followed by full acquisition of the official
2023–2024 archive. `data/processed/california-official-full-originals-v3/`
retains the complete **1,261,848,876-byte** ZIP, SHA-256
`71f098005021971d50c8408bfb1a745d053a633cfa81b3c0d454aa4cdd77b9b6`.
Independent offline replay verifies every one of its **204,862** ZIP entries,
including CRC and uncompressed hashes, and checks local/central metadata for the
5,566 used members. All **5,544 regular-session canonical identities** have
exactly one native table/XML candidate typed `Introduced`/`INT`; no candidate is
missing, ambiguous, identity-conflicting or text-failing within that scope.
Twenty special-session candidates remain separate: nine in session 1 and eleven
in session 2. Intent-only and other previously unselected documents are retained,
not removed to improve source or classification coverage.

The packet contains **5,564 complete raw CAML documents and normalized text
candidates**, totalling **55,860,334 characters**: 9,473,855 header characters,
46,375,351 body characters and 11,128 synthetic joiner characters. Bodies include
4,836 `Bill` and 728 `Resolution` nodes. `introduced_candidates.parquet` records
native version/measure identities, exact raw XML/text hashes and paths;
`canonical_dispositions.parquet` preserves every regular canonical join. All
columns of the six filtered canonical input tables survive separately. Current
canonical titles, abstracts and summaries are not prepended to these inputs.

The **16,692 construction spans** are exactly one header, synthetic `\n\n`
joiner and body per candidate. Character offsets address the normalized Unicode
text; header/body byte offsets and hashes address complete nodes in raw XML.
These are **not typed nested legislative-markup spans**. Text and tails inside
nested XML survive the projection with a synthetic space between each text/tail
fragment before whitespace collapse. Insertion,
deletion, strikeout and other markup semantics are flattened. Deleted words are
not identified or removed. Exact markup remains in `originals/raw_caml/`.
Semantic interpretation remains unfinished. The separate native-element inventory
below now supplies nested source offsets without interpreting them as legal operations.

Only **one new transport request** acquired the archive. The exact original
42,104-byte/65-request ledger prefix remains checksum-bound; aggregate collection
is **66/128**, not a reset allowance. The full HTTP 200 body, raw/parsed headers,
receipt and empty stderr are retained. Transport used ordinary verified HTTPS
without redirect following or certificate bypass. Each successful packet has its
own archive path; hard links share retained bytes but do not require the failed
attempt directories to remain addressable.

`bd5fc67a78c88eeeda65a7b132a7031c52e5968f91eebf205487f872d818ebd6`.

## Full-campaign preparation gates: Pennsylvania, Ohio and Missouri

Pennsylvania's initial immutable preparation and seed-32/seed-30 archives have
actual parent guarded replays. Native population **4,786** remains distinct from
**4,788 canonical contexts**. All **62 complete PDF/HTML pairs** are reused;
the historical **536 exchanges**, **136 over the proposed 400 preparation
ceiling**, the earlier **24/12 follow-up violation** and **12 lost redirect
bodies / 101,094 bytes** remain explicit. The proposed full **25,000** ceiling
includes every inherited exchange, not a new allowance.

Exact-population testing exposed a real v1 grouping defect: restarting after the
provisional seed-tail 30 produces the remaining population's tail 20 instead of
the full population's tail **18**. The immutable v2 supplement fixes carry and
uncheckpointed-completion recovery. Parent guarded replays verify its **580 files**
and separate **195-file final-18 fixture** unchanged; the positive driver fixture
records **4,724 new candidates / 9,448 mocked PDF/HTML responses**, no seed
refetches and genuine dependency-closed final-18 outputs. These are offline
fixtures, not collected Pennsylvania originals.

Full launch is still withheld. Parent code inspection found further v2 problems:
midstream transport errors can discard already observed bytes/headers; HTTP 429
does not stop immediately; a crash between durable reservation append and
progress save can undercount/reuse a reservation; and exhausted or malformed
formats can leave partial candidates while still sealing a normal success-only
tail. The immutable v3 supplement corrects these paths and its positive
full-population fixture retains the genuine final-18 closure. Actual parent
guarded replays pass for the **658-file supplement / 269-file final-18 archive**,
manifest
`2255619fcc1be2d24dccaaf479f787efdf7d00342a5ddc9b8c81671957967eab`.
These fixture inputs remain synthetic, not publisher-collected full-state text.

Parent then actually reproduces **four further v3 release blockers** with
networking and child execution denied: a maintenance HTML shell becomes

HR 2,543; SB 2,633; SCR 62; SJR 93; SR 734. All are preserved rather than
counting only ordinary bills. Exact joins cover **11,807 of 11,809** canonical
regular contexts with no ambiguity. Literal canonical identifiers **SB 15247**
and **SR 1960** remain unmatched; neither is guessed into another identity or
declared nonexistent.

The deterministic initial cohort retains **16 introduced identities**, each with
one complete official PDF and HTML response: **16 pairs / 32 document bodies**.
`document_pages.parquet` has 71 extraction records, but only **55 physical PDF
pages**; the other **16 HTML blocks are not physical pages**. Exact page/text
hashes and combined-text offsets replay independently. Raw HTML preserves nested
tags and attributes; derived text does not retain insertion/deletion, strikeout
or formatting as typed annotations, nor raw-HTML byte offsets. This is bounded
source recovery, not statewide original-text completeness.

Across all attempts collection used **40/128 physical requests**: 35 HTTP 200,
one HTTP 302, one HTTP 500 and three TLS timeouts without an HTTP response.
All received bodies, including the redirect/error, remain byte-bound; zero-byte
timeout records are explicit. Offline replay checks request numbering, pacing,
budget, response bounds and packet-local body lengths/hashes. It validates the
retained TLS declaration and hashed collector, not a new cryptographic handshake.
The original absolute ledger paths are historical observations; verification
uses retained packet-local copies and needs no external transport directory.

Canonical regular tables retain their original columns. Special sessions 881,
882, 883 and 884 are separately retained (117, 169, 598 and 399 contexts), with
disjoint regular/special IDs. Earlier v1/v2 verifier defects remain preserved:
unequal-length strict zip, a zero-count categorical disposition, and bill-only

`c0376c019d06084ec1cf74406d3e7095e90bb493f8aff480994f17b87c9eb9ba`.

## Illinois 103rd-General-Assembly native census and original HTML

The native index retains **13,391 rows**, exactly matching all **13,376**
canonical regular-session contexts without missing or ambiguous canonical
identities. Native type counts are AM 929; EO 12; HB 5,929; HJR 76; HJRCA 22;
HR 989; JSR 2; SB 3,997; SJR 68; SJRCA 14; SR 1,353. Fifteen source-only
entries remain separate: **AM 1020256**, twelve executive orders and JSR 1–2.
No identities are repaired from repository filenames or inferred aliases.

The sixteen selected originals retain **515,168 raw HTML bytes**, **44,158
status-XML bytes**, **168,697 normalized characters** and **2,413 text-bearing
`tr` blocks**. Block spans are zero-based, half-open raw-HTML byte ranges with
exact HTML and hashes. The declared projection decodes entities, normalizes
whitespace, maps `u` to synthetic `[[INSERT:…]]` markers, and maps
`strike`/`s`/`del` to `[[DELETE:…]]`. These are explicit collector rendering
rules, not independently certified interpretation of every underline/strike.

### Offline repair of false native-HTML negatives

Review of the retained responses found three extraction defects: the legacy text
projection includes head CSS/title text, its bare-number identity gate can miss
zero-padded printed identifiers, and its required closed `tr` blocks mistake Word
paragraph-based bills for unavailable HTML. The pinned running collector and its
original dispositions are preserved, not edited in flight.

The successor `illinois_html.py` preserves complete body characters/entities and
mapped `br` newlines, with exact decoded-source/output offsets and literal tag
attributes. It records lexical tokens, not guessed DOM parents: native XSL has
unmatched child tags, and Word bodies need not contain closed table rows. Head
CSS/titles stay outside the projection; struck, underlined and CSS-hidden words
remain present without certified legal or visibility interpretation. Active,
conditional and non-text bodies fail explicitly. A separate anchored printed
GA/year/typed-number observer preserves `HB0015` rather than seeking incidental
bare `15` elsewhere in the bill. This is not legal-version certification.

The immutable diagnostic covers **every negative in the first 51 completed
32-candidate batches**: **578 identity-unverified** and **103 unavailable-shell**
records, plus HB2/HB3 positive controls. All **683 complete native responses**
yield nonempty bodies and matching literal typed headers: **901,346 body
characters / 375,559 lexical spans**. Independent replay reproduces both the
old negative observations and the new complete body text, including a separate
body reader and every source/output span. All **8,991 file hashes** remain
unchanged with networking and original project/session data reads denied.
Twenty-four focused cases and 68 combined IL/TX/NY/CA regressions pass.

Root: `data/processed/illinois-native-html-negative-replay-v1/`.
Manifest SHA-256:
`6d13422e521cf6d0e440ad328974a05d500541bfbead9e962d5db896658e03ef`.
This replay makes **zero requests**, preserves all original campaign states and
does not represent the still-running full campaign as complete. It uses the same
reversible ISO-8859-1 byte projection as the old collector; actual source encoding
is **not certified**, and these are not admitted classifier inputs, labels,
training/evaluation data or analytical/publication evidence.

An additional full-cohort decoding replay establishes a narrower positive result:
**580 responses are entirely ASCII**, and all **103 Word responses explicitly
declare Windows-1252**. Their only non-ASCII byte is `0xA0` (**2,072
occurrences**), which decodes identically under Windows-1252 and Latin-1.
Every response declares only `text/html` in its HTTP headers. Strict
ASCII/declared-Windows-1252 decoding reproduces **every complete source string,
saved body, lexical span and header** without replacement or a guessed codec.
All 8,991 files remain unchanged on both runs. This resolves decoding equivalence
for this fixed cohort, not actual charset ground truth or future responses.

## Official Washington bill-source recovery

The October 19, 2026 recovery retains the complete **24,779** official Washington
bill-version records returned by the Legislature's introduced-since-2017 operation,
**791** historical roster rows across 2017-18 through 2025-26, service documentation
and all eight successful retrieval receipts. Original XML bytes, request scope and hashes
are frozen in `data/processed/washington-official-legislative-recovery-v1/`.
There are **17,472** zero-substitute/zero-engrossment source rows and **17,464** distinct
original bill keys; duplicated originals are retained rather than silently deduplicated.

`data/processed/washington-official-bill-reconciliation-v1/` preserves all **14,143**
baseline Washington measures and joins **13,479** to a unique original official record
using exact session and bill identifiers. An exact biennium, numeric prime-sponsor ID
and original-chamber join then supplies **8,773 D / 4,706 R session-roster observations**.
No fuzzy sponsor-name join, current-roster backfill or invented membership interval is
used. **12,735** matched records include a legal title, retained alongside the official
short/long descriptions and the original baseline title. These are retrieval-time
version-specific source fields, not certified full bill text or filing-time text.

**658** baseline measures lack a unique original in the bulk response, and **six**
have multiple originals. The missing-original population includes **414 SR, three HR,
128 HB, 109 SB, two SJM, one SJR and one SCR**. A sponsor ID can occur in both chambers
after a midterm chamber change; some former-chamber entries are blank. Joining on ID
alone would erase this distinction. The implementation retains chamber boundaries,
blank/duplicate roster uncertainty and every original source-record position.

The **3,979** source-only original records are not automatically missing analytical bills.
They comprise **1,513 gubernatorial appointments**, **2,266 other pre-2018
introductions**, and **200 other post-2017 source-only measures** requiring reconciliation.
These are mutually exclusive descriptive dispositions, not admission decisions. Complete
source-only records and all baseline nonmatches remain downloadable local packet files.

`data/processed/washington-official-reconciliation-verification-v1/` checks every extracted

The combined **25,196** source-version records now yield **13,892** unique original
matches and **9,034 D / 4,858 R session-roster observations**. **245** baseline measures

again checks all 14,143 decisions and every extracted field. No canonical event-time

### Native primary sponsorship and a complete source-session ledger

All **251/251** native sponsorship queries succeeded, preserving **1,997** complete

first returned row or `Order=0`. Unknown primaries cannot be rescued by cosponsors;
unknown sponsor types, chamber conflicts, conflicting parties and missing/ambiguous
roster entries remain unresolved.

### Preventing publisher test records from becoming policy evidence

The classifier now excludes the six exact reviewed Washington system-test/training titles,
with whitespace/case normalization and a Washington-only scope. It does not use a generic
testing keyword. The exclusion applies even with attached sample policy summaries or bodies;
Illinois/New Mexico drafting-shell behavior remains unchanged when substantive evidence exists.
Every excluded row retains its source record, exact classification input/hash and explicit
`source_test_record` reason with `classification_status=excluded`; the model is not invoked.

A frozen replay screens all **14,143** Washington baseline titles and flags **exactly the ten
reviewed source IDs**, with no additional baseline rows flagged. All ten source rows and their
exclusion outputs are preserved in
`data/processed/washington-system-test-exclusion-verification-v1/`.
The substantive HB 2379 title is not excluded. The complete baseline hash remains unchanged.
This checks the bounded reviewed population, not the absence of all other possible test bills
or the accuracy of substantive classifications.

Coverage now reports `n_source_test_excluded` separately, removes these records from the
substantive denominator and does not misreport them as model abstentions. Source/prediction
inventories retain them, and the separate count reaches the state-party atlas and capability
report. Existing coverage artifacts are not silently rewritten; no model training, canonical
analysis or publication admission occurs.

Exclusion verification manifest:
`07dae8272fdb643c7aea25a2bf51ca47ddab7951814a84a8e749893babb626c7`.

### Original-document recovery and a version-note conflict

The completed original-link campaign follows **389 retained references across 249** of
the exact **251** native-recovery targets. **407 requests** include explicit conditional
HTTPS alternatives, yielding **371 retained responses for 240 measures** and **36 HTTP
404 failures**. Every failure belongs to nine previously reviewed system-test records;
the ten test records together include one without an original-note link. These are
bounded request outcomes, not proof that the publisher has no other documents.

Strict extraction retains **241 HTML / 130 PDF** full texts and **1,809 PDF pages**.
The **9,163,509** extracted characters include parallel formats and are not unique policy
volume. All raw bytes, complete text files, source-version/link rows and unsuccessful
receipts remain in immutable packets. **366** automatic native-heading/session matches
cover **237** measures. Five resolution-format exceptions across three more measures
have explicit assistant source-scope reviews, bringing supported original-source context
to **240 measures** without changing any earlier automated result.

Those exceptions are 2017-2018 SR 8678, 2023-2024 SR 8601 and 2025-2026 HR 4607.
SR 8678 has only publisher-path session support, not a fabricated document date.
The 2023 SR 8601 retains deleted earlier years and underlined replacements; plain text
alone cannot distinguish operative from deleted language. HR 4607 uses its native
year-prefixed resolution heading rather than an ordinary bill-cover format.
These are source-scope reviews, not independent semantic or filing-time certification.

The remaining substantive original-note gap, **2019-2020 SR 8601**, has two retained
references, not four. Both official HTML bodies were successfully recovered. The imported
**Substitute Resolution** note conflicts with the first document's plain **SENATE
RESOLUTION 8601** heading. The other document explicitly prints **ENGROSSED SENATE
RESOLUTION E8601**, agreeing with its imported note. Both adoption clauses refer to the
2019 session, but the added rules-heading year is **2018** in the plain-heading version
and **2019** in the engrossed version. Complete texts, native markup, exact source notes,
header excerpts and the discrepancy are preserved separately. A plain heading does not
by itself prove earliest filing-time text; no source note is overwritten or original
version certified from this contrast.

Across these two retrieval packets, **409 requests / 373 retained response bodies**
now provide native documents for **all 241 non-test measures in this bounded cohort**.
This is not 373 unique bills, all-Washington full-text coverage, or automatic admission
to classification. Original-source scope remains supported for 240 measures, with one
explicit version-identity conflict. This text/version issue is separate from **2023-2024
HB 2379's native-primary sponsorship gap**. The detailed API's **245 missing / six
duplicate original-record decisions** remain unchanged; document recovery does not
silently rewrite those metadata results or historical party affiliation.

## All-state document-reference inventory and retrieval

The persistent `state_politics.bills.documents` pipeline now inventories **all 1,090,369**
retained source-context measures, **1,899,909** version records and **2,617,690** links
from the checksum-verified completed October extraction. Every link survives, including
duplicate URLs and later/unclassified version notes. No version records lack a link in
this snapshot. Exact bill/version identity conflicts fail rather than cross-attaching text.

The immutable first pass flags **143,688** whitespace-bearing URLs. Schema-2 successor
v2 preserves each original string while recording a separately encoded request URL,
making syntactically usable references explicit for **37,431 additional measures**.
This is not 37,431 new downloads: v2 has **1,064,790** source-context measures with a
usable request URL, including the ten reviewed publisher tests. It retains **21,252**
measures without a version link and **4,327** with only unsupported FTP references
(**6,522 links**). These are snapshot/reference limitations, not publication absences.
All **752,872 original-note, 439,026 later-note and 1,425,792 unclassified-note links**
remain candidates; original-note hints are not native-version truth.

The reusable collector accepts an explicit manifest-bound link-ID batch, preserves raw
bytes and failure receipts, records HTTPS alternatives separately, skips reviewed source
tests and returns nonzero for unresolved links. An eight-state whitespace-URL smoke
capture retains **seven responses and one Oregon DNS failure**, across **eight receipts /
nine transport attempts**. This is a small retrieval exercise, not coverage certification.

Reading native formats shows why successful requests must not become classifier inputs
automatically. **SD and WY return HTML application shells**, not native bill text; WY's
referenced PDF filename also differs from the baseline chamber identifier and remains
unresolved. **AZ is a proposed strike-everything amendment**, and **UT is an automatic
comparison carrying its own inaccuracy disclaimer**, not an original bill. **NM, OK and
WV** have supported introduced-cover/session contexts in HTML, PDF and DOCX respectively;
DOCX-derived text is explicitly main-document-only.

The Oregon failure was followed through the actual current publisher overview, not
abandoned or repaired by a silent host rewrite. Its native anchors lead to the **2019
SB 9 introduced bill and Senate amendment**, both successfully retained with full
all-page PDF text and separate cover/source reviews. The old DNS failure remains intact.
Complete native responses, full derived text, excerpts, exact notes and review limitations
are frozen; none is independent policy-semantic review or filing-time certification.

### New Mexico native recovery and cross-session quarantine

The bounded New Mexico recovery now retains **all 87 whitespace-bearing references
across 36 source-context bills**, not just introduced-note hints. Its initial **66**
introduced-cover references span **33** bills; all original URLs, notes and parent
responses remain intact. The native-review packet derives **33 HTML / 54 PDF** texts
and attempts embedded extraction across **747 PDF pages**. This is not the complete
New Mexico legislative universe or full independent semantic review.

Two attached **Final Version** PDFs are demonstrably from the wrong session. Each
2024 special-session attachment is byte-identical to the final linked by the official
**regular-session** overview's actual **Final Version 3/04/24** anchor:

| Special-session bill | Introduced-cover subject | Misattached regular-session final |
|---|---|---|
| SB 6 | Fentanyl penalties | Cannabis regulation |
| SB 17 | Crime and ballistic reporting | Hospital assessment and health-care delivery |

The regular overviews, native session headings, exact anchor evidence and four comparison
receipts are preserved. Neither bill-number equality nor an HTTP-success response is
enough to establish bill/session identity. The reusable collector now retains but
quarantines the **exact reviewed bill-ID/response-hash pairs**, with a native-review
manifest binding. An actual fresh two-link CLI run retained both responses, reported
exactly two quarantines and no transport-unresolved links, and returned **1** for those
quarantines. Legitimate regular-session contexts and changed publisher bytes are not
blanket-blocked; they remain unreviewed and uncertified.

The other **19 nonoriginal references** retain native excerpts and extraction limitations,
not automatic version certification. One, **2026 HB 99 `OLD.pdf`**, has visible policy
text but both independent embedded extractors return only a 61-character heading across
23 pages. A separate pinned-Tesseract successor recovers **33,501 OCR characters on all
23 pages**, retaining every rendered raster and per-page output. Rendered pages **1, 2
and 23** were visually inspected, not every policy provision. The native heading identifies
a **House Judiciary Committee substitute**, and the publisher warns that amendments
may be proposed but not adopted. Original PDF styling remains necessary: OCR loses
strikeout, underscore and color distinctions. Neither the filename nor the printed
document timestamp establishes filing-time or operative-version status.

and source order for 2,617,690 links**, **all six fields for 1,899,909 version records**,
every state's reference totals, the 1,090,369-row bill coverage count and unchanged extraction
hashes. It confirms the **37,431-measure request-usability gain** without claiming new

### Native shell resolution, subject changes and mirror-bill evidence

The successor `data/processed/sd-wy-native-document-resolution-v1/` follows retained
publisher application code and configuration to the actual public APIs and document
links. Its **23 successful follow-up responses** preserve the client/API provenance,
**nine PDFs and all 96 pages**, including separate pinned OCR and rasters for all six
image-only veto pages. This resolves the two bounded application-shell observations;
it is not a statewide completeness finding.

For **SD 2018 HB 1132**, the native regular-session record and complete four-version
list recover the introduced PDF and three later engrossed PDFs. The introduced subject
is the alcohol-consumption age for military service members. All three later versions
instead concern alcoholic-beverage sponsorship/advertising and explicitly warn of
extensive amendment ("hoghoused"). The baseline and current native summary title match
the later subject, not introduction. A title-only classifier can therefore assign the
wrong filing-time policy even when the source title is faithfully imported. Both subjects,
every native version and the original title remain retained; no title is overwritten.
Two native primary-sponsor Republican observations are session-scoped, not certified
party-at-filing assignments.

For **WY 2020S1 SF 1004**, the native Senate API explicitly records **"S See Mirror Bill
HB1004"** and itself lists the obsolete House veto link present in the baseline. The
chamber mismatch is thus publisher-listed related material, **not a demonstrated import
error**. The Senate introduced text and the separate House introduced, enrolled, veto
letter and final-veto-summary PDFs are retained under their correct contexts. The House
bill has its own baseline identity; its documents are not silently reassigned as Senate
versions or treated as proof of the Senate bill's enacted status. Scanned-veto pages 1
and 6 were visually inspected, not all six; noisy OCR does not certify amounts, deletions
or operative provisions. The final summary also warns that its pagination differs from
the original enrolled act.

The imported-note parser now recognizes plural **"Amendments"** and **"Final Version"**
as later-version hints, without promoting any source to native-version or filing-time
certification. Inventory **v3** preserves every original link, version field, URL,
eligibility decision and row order from v2. Only **15,146** previously unclassified
link hints change, spanning **11,870** source-context measures in **nine** states;
all corresponding per-bill and per-state counts reconcile. A separate programmatic
successor also checks all five bill identity/context/title fields and original row order
against the unchanged 1,090,369-row source, preserving the preceding full link/version
reconciliation. These are metadata corrections, not new document recoveries or independent
semantic validation.

### South Dakota native-universe reconciliation

The completed `data/processed/south-dakota-native-universe-reconciliation-v1/` retains
the publisher's **43-session** inventory and complete bill-list responses for all
**14** published 2018-2026 sessions: **nine regular and five special**. The new recovery
has **18 successful retained requests**, with the earlier client-source evidence copied
separately. All **5,366** South Dakota baseline rows and every original column/order
survive. The native lists contain **5,369** records: **5,365** exact session/identifier
matches, **four native-only measures**, and **one unmatched baseline identity**.

The four native-only rows are `2021i` **HR 7002 and SR 701/702/703**, concerning impeachment
and Senate trial proceedings. Their introduction observations are in **2022**, but the
publisher explicitly assigns them to **2021i**. They must not be moved to the 2022 regular
session by a calendar-year join. Across the native universe, **199** introduction-year
prefixes precede the session year and **four** follow it; missing observations remain
missing, not inferred event dates. These are native-list observations, not independently
adjudicated temporal or legal findings.

Following the unmatched **2022 HJR 1** source recovered the actual native bill **22633**,
its introduced-version record and all **three PDF pages**, whose header identifies
**HJR 5001**. The baseline already contains a separate HJR 5001 row. Both baseline
identities explicitly cite the same native overview/API record; the HJR 1 row carries the
introduced PDF links while HJR 5001 has no retained version links but does have action/vote
metadata. This is a split/alias identity requiring reconciliation, **not evidence of two
separate native measures**. Both original records, their different metadata, all four
overview/API source rows and the document references remain retained without silently
merging, deleting or renumbering either source identity.

The reusable resolver in `bills/south_dakota.py` uses exact native session keys and
explicit type/number fields. It retains duplicate native rows, exposes ambiguous source
or native identities instead of choosing the first match, rejects incomplete/conflicting
response scope, and keeps every source-only row. A separate raw-response replay checks
every original native record/position, every baseline decision and the complete source-only
population. All **5,365** matched current titles are exact matches; this does **not**
resolve the introduced-versus-later-title problem demonstrated above.

Manifest: `6d104c4c032e3e64daacc7b7ae842ed86adf89578ac9c2c2c598557a4e9ca384`.
This is retrieval-time native-list reconciliation, not certified analytical completeness,
filing-time text/party recovery or independent semantic review. Native listed document IDs
do not establish version identity. New source-only records remain separate, canonical files
are unchanged, and the companion remains unpublished and unintegrated. Complete bytes,
tables, receipts and producer scripts are local Git-ignored evidence, not remotely backed
up by pushing code and manifest identities.

Canonical audit **v22** verifies **47** research packets, retains actual return code **1**
and reports **34/328 failures**. Only the passing/code-documentation check counts changed

### Version-isolated bill inputs and complete SD input replay

The bill classifier previously concatenated current title, available summary and
introduced text without establishing their version alignment. This is unsafe even
when all three fields come from the same native bill. **2018 SD HB1132 / BillId 8918 /
DocumentId 47568** supplies a retained concrete counterexample: its introduced text
concerns military drinking-age eligibility, but its current native title concerns
alcohol sponsorship/advertising agreements.

`build_bill_input()` now uses **nonblank introduced text alone**. Title plus available
summary remains the fallback only when introduced text is absent or whitespace-only.
Insufficient introduced text abstains rather than being rescued by unrelated metadata.
The source columns remain unchanged; exact input text/hash, selected fields and
`introduced_text_only_else_title_summary_v2` construction identify the model input.
This does not certify timing, native identity, extraction quality or a label.
Reviewed Washington publisher-test exclusions still apply before inference.

A second repaired traceability defect discarded the model's ordered `output_codes`
and `rejection_score` while projecting bill predictions into the fixed export schema.
Both now survive classification and state-partition Parquet export alongside complete
scores/logits and abstention reasons. Unscored/baseline records keep these unavailable,
not fabricated. Focused regressions cover version-isolated text, exact whitespace/hashes,
insufficient introduced evidence, fallback compatibility and end-to-end learned-rejection
exports; the combined bill/classifier/document checks pass **164 tests**.

The new unlabelled candidate inventory accounts for **all 5,369 native SD introduced
documents / 15,308 distinct physical pages**. It retains **10,738 alternative inputs**,
one per document and reader, with complete page text, exact source/input spans, per-page
hashes, source metadata and shared native-bill groups. Alternatives are not extra bills
or independent split candidates. No current title/summary is prepended to a PDF input.

### Native Montana universe and draft/formal identity reconciliation

The largest apparent no-document-URL population needed an identity audit before more
text collection: all **7,641** Montana rows without usable retained URLs have LC
identifiers. The official 2024 drafting manual, physical page 65, distinguishes the
LC request number used during drafting from the House/Senate number assigned at
introduction. An LC source record does **not** establish another introduced filing,
but neither does an LC identifier establish that its underlying request was never
introduced.

The current statistics and drafting-procedure URLs returned HTTP 200 **maintenance
pages**, not statistics or procedural evidence. Their complete bodies and receipts
remain retained with that disposition. The actual Bill Explorer HTML links a public
client bundle that declares separate archive/current search APIs and session registries.
Following those exact contracts recovered all **15,830 native records across 18 complete
pages**, explicitly selecting the 2019, 2021, 2023 and 2025 regular sessions. The registry's
future 2027 cohort remains in the original response but is not admitted to this window.
Every POST body, URL, retrieval receipt, native object and page position is preserved.

| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |

| 2023 | 4,643 | 1,698 | 6,335 |
| 2025 | 4,495 | 1,759 | 6,159 |
| Total | 15,830 | 6,079 | 18,403 |

The native `offset` parameter is a **page index**, not a row offset. Retained diagnostic
requests using `limit=100&offset=100` report page 100 and physical offset 10,000 with
empty content for both API families. Complete collection instead enumerates page
indices, verifies declared totals, sizes, first/last flags and unique LC identities,
and uses bounded 1,000-row pages to reduce requests. Copying the client bundle's
row-offset-style loops would silently skip source records.

The new `bills/montana.py` reconciler preserves every original baseline field/order,
full native JSON and LC/formal aliases. **18,399** baseline rows have unique native

similar titles or silently renumbered. **4,585 LC baseline rows** share a native record

not treated as a reliable measure-stage contract.

Of the 7,641 no-URL LC rows, **7,488** match native records with no formal identity,

### Montana printed identities contradict simple alias merging

with M. Yakawich named. Their source overviews reference LC0239 and LC0442, respectively.

identities, with A. Regier and V. Moore named, respectively. The unnumbered LC0239 and
LC0442 drafts are retained too.

This is **not proof that either Senate identifier is an erroneous source alias**.
Nor does a numbered print or an `INTRODUCED BY` line prove an introduction event,
its date, or continuity with the current House record. Similar policy text, shared LC
references, PDF metadata dates and current enactment status cannot adjudicate that
history. All four original exact-key nonmatches remain unchanged; no Senate/House
record was merged, deleted or admitted to analytical filing counts.

### Montana LC source rows excluded as additional formal filings

The filing consumers now share `exclude_mt_lc_source_requests_v1`, a narrow source-identity
guard rather than an introduction-status adjudication. An exact Montana LC request row
is not an additional independent formal filing, even when imported classification says
`bill`. This does **not** establish that its underlying request was never introduced,
delete any source row or evidence, label LC3368 a publisher test, or merge the contradictory
SB6/HB117 and SB85/HB161 formal records. Montana input frames missing a nonempty source
identifier fail explicitly.

The guard is wired through revealed emphasis, recorded outcomes, election shares and
tag validation, title-based terms, exact/near reuse **before candidate blocking**, and
direct source-tag validation/replication/trends. LC source-request rows do not reach the classifier.
Record exports preserve original fields and exact assembled input/hash, with
`source_draft_request`, an explicit `excluded` status and reason, and no model scores.
Coverage reports `n_source_draft_excluded`, retains the original attributed source-row
count and excludes requests from substantive/classified/abstention denominators.
State-focus and capability evidence carry the exclusion count.

The separate source census binds **all 1,090,369 source rows** and preserves all
**18,403 complete Montana contexts**. It identifies **12,321 LC request source rows**:
**7,343** pass the old classification-only outcome predicate; **12,321** pass the
election measure-type predicate. These are latent scope exposures, **not thousands
of demonstrated published double-counts**. Only 60 source rows nationally currently
carry D/R attribution, and **none is in Montana**. The guard therefore removes
**zero currently party-attributed rows**. Its effects on unattributed title-reuse
candidates were not quantified here.

All **12,321** request rows were exported as complete, unscored exclusions. Independent
replay re-reads the unchanged canonical source, reconstructs every national scope flag
without the producer's matcher, independently reconstructs both measure-type predicates,
and checks every source field, input text/hash, exclusion status and empty score/chunk
record. It also reproduces the session/classification accounting. No model was loaded

interrupted v1 already consumed **12 transport requests**, and v2 incorrectly

redirect payloads; complete-body coverage is audited separately across both attempts.
No further network requests are authorized by an attempt restart. Future retries
must reuse retained evidence and share one aggregate request budget across roots.

## Training reservation enforcement

The training CLI now accepts an explicit `--source-reservation` pointing to a
manifest-bound reservation JSON. Before importing model libraries or downloading weights,
it verifies every packet file and excludes reserved platform-training organizations,
including alternate editions, as well as matching body or complete-document text hashes.
State/party metadata, explicit organization identifiers and organization-based groups must
agree. Reviewed state-platform training rows require an organization identity; external
national-platform groups do not silently become state organizations. Bills and reserved
evaluation rows are not excluded by platform-training reservations.

When supplied, the exact reservation path, JSON hash, packet-manifest hash and organization
list enter the saved training manifest. Omission is recorded as null; older runs are not
retroactively described as having used this guard. The six reserved organizations remain
ID-R, MT-R, WA-R, MN-D, WA-D and VT-D. At that checkpoint no labels or weights changed.
The subsequent reviewed use of complete retained KS-R, NE-R and HI-R native documents
is recorded below; their original source-context packet remains unlabelled evidence.

`data/labels/platform-challenge-reserved-sources-v1/` froze complete original bytes and

predictions. The reservation applies to those organizations' **other document versions
too**: they must not subsequently become training evidence for an allegedly source-disjoint
comparison. These five states are outside all existing platform training/development
states. Availability does not establish contemporary operative adoption or permission.

`11d22bd486e2bcd46eaa1171fb1090cea12c682290674c2e6c044b7e00a2deae`.

mixed-policy rejections and six non-policy front-matter/navigation rejections. The
population is **28 D / 24 R**; positives are **23 D / 19 R**. Both technology references
and both culture/arts references are Democratic, not evidence of per-topic party balance.
Source counts are MN-D 15, WA-R 10, MT-R 8, VT-D 8, ID-R 6 and WA-D 5.

All **6,112** preceding training/development records remain unchanged. Exact offsets,
original text/body/document hashes, complete source parquets, native bytes/text, nearby
review context and rationales are retained. Explicit native boundaries prevent the
space-only blank lines in Vermont's HTML extraction from combining unrelated commitments.
Cross-split identities and close-unit copies are checked; scored source spans do not
overlap. Three additional units remain unscored: AI infrastructure/federalism, marriage
recognition/federalism and a combined civil-rights/health paragraph. The packet's **23**
bound files and every selected/context span independently reconcile. Manifest:
`b125ba94eb2a77fb2ab48b22ba84e8c3cadef0b286784f603064ee2944796fb8`.

The comparison plan freezes the same three saved models, their actual original training
labels and checkpoint-file hashes, plus the unchanged research-default policy
(`temperature=1.0`, confidence floor `0.8`, margin floor `0.2`) **before predictions**.
Plan: `data/processed/source-disjoint-platform-comparison-v1/evaluation_plan.json`,
hash `f57b0816620aad8e0f0cf1e57e993970146ed1c3bd6622ee9ac5e7acb9d3a54e`.
No calibration, further training or model promotion is authorized. This is a purposive
source-disjoint challenge, **not independent human gold, a random sample, representative
population accuracy or a release certification**. Two positives per topic and six source
clusters cannot substantiate stable topic/subgroup performance. Source-family dependence
and the adapted family/moral rubric remain unresolved.

The actual fixed-policy comparison completed for all three saved checkpoints:

| Challenge result | External-only | Joint target/rejection | Broader-source curriculum |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Correct decisions | 41/52 | 47/52 | 47/52 |
| Correctly accepted policy references | 40/42 | 40/42 | 41/42 |
| Correctly rejected mixed units | 0/4 | 1/4 | 1/4 |
| Correctly rejected non-policy units | 1/6 | 6/6 | 5/6 |
| Democratic references, correct decisions | 23/28 | 26/28 | 26/28 |
| Republican references, correct decisions | 18/24 | 21/24 | 21/24 |

The broader model fixes the general scientific-research reference previously assigned
international affairs, but newly accepts Minnesota's publication-description front matter
as government operations with score **0.922**. Those are the only changes in correctness
relative to the joint model, so the wider challenge does **not** demonstrate an aggregate
improvement. A Montana ranked-choice/primary-design policy is withheld by all three
models under the fixed confidence floor; its provisional reference is topic 20.

Both tax/trade and economic deregulation/labor mixtures are still forced into topic 1,
and a parent bullet combining banking, taxation, workforce programs and antitrust is
forced into topic 15. The broader model's scores for these three false accepts are
**0.999997, 0.999950 and 0.992607**. High uncalibrated scores are not safeguards against
lossy single-topic assignments. It accepts 45/52 cases; only **41/45** accepted decisions
match the provisional references. The mixed/non-policy failures must not disappear behind
the 41/42 accepted-policy result.

## Complete sponsorship-attribution missingness audit

The retained dump contains **21,993 party memberships**, of which **21,810 are entirely
undated**. Only **163 people** have any dated membership; 54 also have undated records,
and none of those mixed dated/undated people has only one recorded party. The bounds
contain no partial-length dates that a relaxed parser could safely rescue. None of
5,936 organization sponsorships names an explicit party organization in the retained
membership reference. These checked alternatives do not repair historical coverage.

The shared temporal resolver now exposes explicit evidence/missingness reasons while
preserving the existing `party_on_date()` interface and every party decision. Future
bill extraction also writes `party_resolution_reason`; current-party labels and
legislative-tenure dates are not substituted for missing party dates.

A complete replay preserves every original cell of **5,028,893 sponsorship rows** and adds
the reason, with **zero party or source-cell mismatches**:

| Resolution reason | All sponsorship rows | Recorded-primary rows |
|---|---:|---:|
| Undated membership | 4,257,879 | 2,052,642 |
| Missing person ID | 640,763 | 404,536 |
| Missing event date | 119,935 | 22,113 |
| Nonpartisan legislature | 10,014 | 8,317 |
| Active dated membership | 302 | 278 |

These are rows, not unique bills or people. Multiple primary sponsors can occur on one
bill. Reasons reflect the resolver's priority order: an undated membership can prevent
a later conflict check, so absence of a conflict reason is not proof of consistent
affiliation history. Missing person IDs also include source non-person sponsors; the
original entity type, name and organization fields remain present for further review.
The canonical bills still have only **39 D and 21 R** assignments under these rules;
unresolved co-primary sponsors cannot be rescued by resolved cosponsors.

The complete successor table, state/primary reason CSV, frozen code/tests and input hashes
are in `data/processed/bill-party-attribution-diagnostic-v1/`. Manifest:
`2f2406f260205225c3ff25ae14ea10ee9ba6ebabfd5bfb320b8538d331ff40ac`.
All **61** focused bill/outcome regressions pass. The current-legislator and methods
documentation now correctly distinguish current inventory from historical affiliation,
source-context years from filing dates, and no-primary fallback from unresolved-primary
backfill. No canonical source rows, bill party assignments or analytical results changed.
The source gap requires additional historical evidence, not weakening the join.

## Full-document text split isolation

Nine reproduced failures exposed a narrower remaining leak: distinct platform body
hashes could carry identical complete document text into different splits, while malformed
optional document hashes were accepted. The shared loader now validates provided
`document_text_sha256` values and checks this additional identity across both complete
training/evaluation manifests. The same guard applies before development calibration,
including original development documents later presented as independent tests.
Same-split platform passages and distinct bills sharing snapshot metadata remain valid.
All **68** ModernBERT regressions pass after the fix.

Full-document hashes remain optional for historical label compatibility; source-support
reports explicitly count missing values. This does not certify byte-distinct copies
without that metadata, differently extracted versions or near-duplicate families.
It does not infer a full-document identity from one selected passage.

`data/processed/training-evaluation-lineage-v2/` freezes both saved pilots rescored against
the complete metadata-only development successor and their exact original training evidence.
Both still make **27/50** decisions under unchanged policies. The ten selected platform
rows have source-bound full-document hashes; missing hashes in original training-manifest
development rows remain visible rather than being rewritten.
No weights, original labels, logits or thresholds changed. This is a provenance safeguard,
not an accuracy improvement or independent evaluation.
Manifest: `98952c935fc0553546ce4ddf8dc6dd0fc542b1f229dc3775f136e8266efc6e2c`.

## Platform-specific support and metadata audit

The pooled **27/50** development result conceals domain differences. Reusing the exact
saved logits, original references and unchanged research-default decision policies yields:

| Provisional development decisions | External-only pilot | Target/rejection pilot |
|---|---:|---:|
| Bills, 40 cases | 22/40 | 23/40 |
| Platform passages, 10 cases | 5/10 | 4/10 |
| Pooled, 50 cases | 27/50 | 27/50 |

These counts include correct abstentions. Among platform cases, correctly accepted policy
decisions fall from four to two; correctly rejected cases rise from one to two.
Neither ten passages from one document nor provisional assistant judgments establish
population accuracy. The platform result is not an improvement simply because bill
decisions improve or the pooled score stays constant.

The joint pilot's **15** reviewed target-platform training rows comprise **six positive
spans across five of 21 topics and nine rejections**. All come from two **Democratic**
documents: California and Texas. The ten platform development rows all come from one
Texas **Republican** document. This is sparse, party/source-confounded support, not a
representative cross-state benchmark; it cannot isolate a party effect from document/topic
selection. Another run on the unchanged corpus cannot repair
the missing Republican target-platform training sources, sixteen unsupported topics,
limited geography or absent independent test/challenge references.

The original platform development rows omitted party and direct extraction-file metadata.
A separate complete **50-row** successor restores these fields for the ten platform rows:
party `R`, extraction path/hash and complete document-text hash. All **25** reviewed
training/development platform spans match their retained document extractions exactly,
and their three PDF body hashes reconcile with retained inventory captures. Topic,
rejection, rationale, input text/hash and original prediction bytes are unchanged; all
pooled and domain metrics remain identical. Bill party fields remain unknown rather than
being inferred from bill content. Original training labels/manifests remain immutable.

New training manifests and evaluation reports now expose per-split/domain external and
reviewed target support for every topic, reviewed rejection counts, platform-body
identities, recorded state/party and missing metadata. Explicit zero-row cells prevent
absent domains or held-out splits from disappearing from the audit. Counts describe
available supervision, not label correctness, independence or release eligibility.
All **59** ModernBERT regressions pass.

Current source-bound packet: `data/processed/platform-label-support-audit-v2/`;
manifest `847989d20a0009158fa5689a2b065d671cee5fe81a4c83602e24c071b1a84993`.
The preserved v1 contains the same valid metadata/metric findings but was frozen before

training rerun, source adoption finding or publication.

## Checkpoint-bound evaluation lineage

Separate evaluation files can no longer bypass the training split guard. Both evaluation
and calibration now require the original checkpoint manifest and the complete label file
whose byte hash it records. The prediction's base-model run, training-label bytes and
taxonomy must match before the shared isolation check compares both complete manifests.
It checks record IDs, groups, normalized input and reviewed platform-body hashes, including
original development sources that must not later become independent test evidence.
Shared legislative snapshots still do not collapse distinct bills. Missing or mismatched
training provenance fails closed before a metrics/calibration report is created.

All **58** ModernBERT regressions pass, including ten new separate-file cases covering
each leakage identity, calibration, development-to-test reuse, altered labels/model/
taxonomy bindings and the legitimate shared-bill-snapshot exception. The unchanged real
pilot logits were rescored against their exact original training manifests in
`data/processed/training-evaluation-lineage-v1/`: both pass lineage checks and remain
**27/50**, using their unchanged research-default decisions. Original labels, weights,
logits, reports and thresholds were not rewritten. This verifies provenance isolation,
not label correctness, improved accuracy, independent gold or publication readiness.
Packet manifest: `598a88dcd7f050c134840d295a875eb295166243d478f4eb71fbae453c8cd4c7`.

## Initial platform-document split guard

The label loader previously accepted distinct passages from the same platform body in
different splits when their group IDs differed. A reproduced regression now fails closed:
reviewed target-platform rows require a source-body SHA-256, and a shared body cannot
cross train/dev/test/challenge splits, regardless of URL or group alias. Same-split
passages remain valid; multiple bills in one source snapshot are not collapsed into
one document. Preparation, training and evaluation all use this shared loader.

Both complete frozen pilot manifests still validate: **5,945** external-only rows and
**6,051** joint target/rejection rows. No old labels, predictions, scores or weights
changed, and no new training run was started. The ModernBERT test suite passes **48**
cases at that checkpoint. The initial check applied only to rows loaded together; it did
not establish disjointness
between a separately supplied evaluation file and a checkpoint's training corpus.
Byte-distinct versions, paraphrases, source-family review and independent gold remain
unresolved. The checkpoint-bound successor above closes the separate-file gap without
retroactively changing the original reports.

## Preserved v5 uniform-registry recovery checkpoint

At this checkpoint the immutable recovery inventory was **v5**, and the local preview was
`data/preview-recovery-v4/`. Every field and alternate extraction in the original
488-record v4 stream is preserved exactly. The successor has **1,452 request records,
1,346 retained responses, 106 failures and 1,296 distinct response-body hashes**.
All 100 organizations now have capture records; 97 have a retained response in this
recovery union. Arizona Democrats, Illinois Democrats and Oklahoma Republicans do not.
Those three are recovery-response gaps, not evidence that no platform or policy exists;
the historical archive remains a separate evidence layer.

The uniform campaign accounts for 631 requests: 100 registered homepages, one HTTPS
follow-up, 191 first-hop navigation requests, 91 second-hop requests and 248 document
requests. It retained 605 responses, including 237 actual PDFs, and 26 failures.
Offline reconciliation checked every capture/body/text hash, receipt, organization count,
parent binding and all 6,471 observed references. Reconstructing recorded URL resolution
found no corrected relative targets. The bounded crawl is not an exhaustive website search.

All **296 distinct still-unretained deferred direct-document targets** were subsequently
requested, rather than only high-scoring candidates. These produced 251 retained responses,
250 actual PDFs and 45 failures, all in the South Carolina Republican follow-up. Exact
selection/parent reconciliation binds every target to its original observation and frozen
plan. Older resolutions, county/district bylaws, minutes, roll calls and forms are included:
these counts must not be described as newly recovered platforms. Deferred navigation
references remain outside this document follow-up.

A separate adapter reconciles **37 actual historical request receipts**, not extra copies
of selected-fetch pointers, attempts arrays or reviewed texts: three California/Texas PDF
requests, 20 gap-review/retention requests, ten Pennsylvania follow-up-v1 requests and four
follow-up-v2 requests. There are 21 retained responses and 16 failures. Original selected
texts, including the pilot PDFs' layout extraction, remain unchanged; new extraction from
another retained receipt is explicitly distinguished. Pennsylvania's Fall 2025 minutes
request remains an HTTP-200 owner-permission-error HTML shell, **not minutes or DOCX**.
No restriction was bypassed. Legacy manifests use specific hash fields; previously
unhashed receipt files are frozen now, not falsely claimed historically hash-pinned.

### Extraction quality and institutional boundaries

The uniform PDFs reproduced warnings on 35 captures. Ten had empty native extraction;
Montana Republican bylaws had nonempty native text but explicit incomplete-font warnings.
A separate derivative recovers these eleven sources across 94 physical pages. Two further
substantive scans add three pages: a South Carolina party resolution and a Texas governor
letter. **All 97 new OCR page renders and transcriptions were independently reproduced**,
using the restored declared Tesseract 5.5.2. Large Florida map pages have explicitly reduced
DPI under a 20-million-pixel limit; OCR does not preserve district geometry.

Five limited assistant source-role reviews bind seven visually inspected page renders and
the full extracted county delegate procedure. They are not gold labels or certification:

- South Carolina's resolution contains substantive endorsement language; its displayed
  page does not establish an adoption date, and OCR drops list numbers 2–5.
- Texas's July 6, 2021 letter is from the governor to the PUC, not a state-party platform
  merely because a party index links it. OCR omits its signature/name block.
- New Mexico's two-page ballot graphic encodes recommendations spatially. Plain OCR of
  FOR/AGAINST labels is not reliable policy-direction evidence.
- Montana's inspected cover and first substantive page report June 29, 2024 adoption/
  amendment, without independent confirmation. OCR corrupts contents leaders/numerals.
- Idaho's separately recovered DOCX is a Canyon County delegate procedure and unfilled
  affiliation pledge, not statewide policy. Main-document XML is not layout or final-state
  certification.

The 34 other empty-native PDFs in the deferred packet remain explicit extraction gaps,
primarily older resolutions and administrative material. Original native text is never
overwritten. Successor builds reuse inherited typed alternatives for repeated bytes only
within the same state-party organization, reject conflicting extraction identities and
require the actual 50-state × D/R registry. The browser shows recorded OCR runtimes,
unknown row-level runtimes and the incomplete-font warning without calling DOCX OCR.

The isolated declared runtime is `data/tools/ocr-5.5.2/`. Historical 5.5.3 derivatives and
`data/tools/ocr/` remain preserved; restoring the runtime does not relabel those outputs.
The current full audit using 5.5.2 fails **34 of 317 checks**, all explicit missing canonical
analytical inputs/results. The earlier default-PATH 35/316 result below is historical.
Neither ModernBERT candidate is approved; the unchanged 27/50 development result is not
improved by downloading text. No labels, weights, cutoff or analytical admission changed.
